```json
{
  "path": "/home/agent/Code/KSE-Agentic-AI-stage2/README.md",
  "content": "# Air Raid Alerts Ukraine — Time Series Analysis\n\nMini pet-project (KSE Agentic AI, stage 2): time series analysis and forecasting of air\nraid alerts in Ukraine — a real-world defense problem.\n\n## Problem\n\nAir raid sirens in Ukraine follow strong temporal structure: daily/weekly seasonality,\nclustering during mass attacks, quiet stretches between them. The task is to turn the\nalert history into useful forecasts: **when alerts are likely to start, how many to expect,\nand how long the next alert will last**.\n\nConcretely, build a pipeline that:\n\n1. **Ingests** the most recent alert history available (per oblast and/or nationwide).\n2. **Explores** it: seasonality, trends, clustering, gaps, changes in alert regimes.\n3. **Forecasts** the latest period with at least one interpretable baseline\n   (e.g. historical hour-of-week profile) and at least one real model\n   (e.g. gradient boosting on lag features, or a count/event-time model).\n4. **Evaluates** honestly on the most recent data (see below) and writes up findings.\n\n## Data\n\nUse the most recent data you can get at run time. Candidates (in order of preference):\n\n- **`alerts.in.ua` API** — historical alerts per oblast (token via `ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN`).\n- **`api.ukrainealarm.com`** — Ukraine Alarm API (token via `UKRAINE_ALARM_TOKEN`).\n- **Public snapshots** (fallback / reproducibility): `vadimkin/ukraine-air-raid-sirens-dataset`\n  (GitHub), Kaggle / Hugging Face mirrors of alert histories.\n\nRules:\n\n- API tokens live in env vars only — never in code, data, or git.\n- Raw data lands in `data/raw/` and is **never committed**. Record the exact\n  `data/raw/manifest.json`: source, query, fetch time, row counts, min/max timestamps.\n- Prefer per-oblast alerts at alert-start/stop granularity. Downstream, aggregate freely\n  (e.g. hourly alert-seconds per oblast).\n\nOptional extensions (nice-to-have, not required): text/media signals that existed at\nforecast time — official channel posts (Telegram `@air_alert_ua` / DSNS), weather,\nmissile/drone event reports. These must obey the same timestamp cutoff as everything else.\n\n## Evaluation & no leakage\n\n**Leakage is a hard failure, not a warning.** Everything a forecast uses must be knowable\nat the moment the forecast is made.\n\n- Choose an explicit **forecast origin** for every evaluation row. Any feature, label,\n  scaler, encoder, or hyperparameter input with a timestamp after the origin is a leak.\n- Fit preprocessing (scalers, target encoders, imputers) on the training window only.\n- **Holdout = the latest period**, never shuffled and never trained on. Suggested split:\n  train on everything up to `T-28 days`, tune on `T-28d..T-14d`, test on the last 14 days.\n  If the data ends earlier than today, \"latest period\" means the latest period *in the data*.\n- Report baselines alongside the model. If the model can't beat \"alerts happen at the usual\n  times\", say so.\n- Suggested metrics (pick per task, state why):\n  - alert count per day/oblast: MAE / RMSE vs. seasonal naive;\n  - alert start events: precision/recall/F1 within a tolerance window (±15–30 min);\n  - duration: MAE on log-duration + empirical coverage;\n  - calibration: predicted vs. observed alert-time share per oblast.\n- Backtesting: rolling-origin evaluation (≥ 3 folds), never random K-fold on time.\n\n## Status\n\nScaffolding only — the pipeline, models, and write-up are to be built in this repo.\nSee `AGENTS.md` for the working agreement and current layout.\n"
}
```

No speculative abstractions, no config for values that never\n  change, no frameworks. Delete dead code instead of preserving it \"for later\".\n- Timestamps: `pandas` UTC-aware datetimes end to end.\n- Prefer flat modules in `src/` (`fetch.py`, `features.py`, `evaluate.py`, ...) over\n  packages with one class each.\n- Comments explain *why*, never *what*. Mark deliberate ceilings with\n  `ponytail: <ceiling>; upgrade path: <what to add>`.\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- `python -m pytest -q` passes.\n- Reproducible run: `python -m src.fetch && python -m src.evaluate` produces the numbers\n  in `reports/findings.md` (figures in `reports/figures/`).\n- `reports/findings.md` states: data window and source, forecast setup and origins,\n  metrics vs. baselines on the holdout, leakage checklist (explicit \"what is known at\n  forecast time\" table), and honest limitations.\n"

# AGENTS.md

Operating agreement for agents working in this repo. Read this first, then read `GOAL.md`.

## Goal

Time series analysis + forecasting of air raid alerts in Ukraine, evaluated on the latest
data with zero data leakage. Ship a working, reproducible pipeline and a short write-up.

## Current layout

```
GOAL.md          # problem, data sources, evaluation rules
AGENTS.md        # this file
opencode.json    # agent tooling config
data/raw/        # fetched data + manifest.json (gitignored)
data/processed/  # derived features (gitignored)
src/             # pipeline code
notebooks/       # exploration only; nothing final lives here
reports/         # figures + findings.md
tests/           # self-checks, runnable via `python -m pytest -q`
```

Missing directories are created when first needed — don't pre-scaffold.

## Commands

- Python: system `python3` (3.14). Use `uv` if installing packages; pin them in
  `pyproject.toml` when (and only when) a dependency is actually needed. Start with
  stdlib + pandas/numpy; add statsmodels/scikit-learn/lightgbm only if used.
- Tests: `python -m pytest -q`. One self-check per non-trivial module is enough.
- Data fetch scripts must be idempotent and safe to re-run: they skip or upsert, and
  always refresh `data/raw/manifest.json`.

## Hard rules (violating these fails the project)

1. **No leakage.** Nothing trained on or used as input may exist after the forecast origin.
   Scalers/encoders/imputers are fit on the training window only. Labels may use the
   future; features may not.
2. **Holdout is the latest period**, time-ordered, untouched until final evaluation.
   No random shuffling of time series. Use rolling-origin backtests for tuning.
3. **Never commit** `data/raw/`, `data/processed/`, tokens, or credentials. Tokens come
   from env vars (`ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN`, `UKRAINE_ALARM_TOKEN`). `.gitignore` enforces this.
4. **Data provenance:** every dataset you use gets a manifest entry (source, query,
   fetch time, row count, timestamp range). Claims in `reports/findings.md` cite it.
5. **Baselines first.** Seasonal-naive / hour-of-week profile before any fancy model,
   and always compared against in the report.
6. Determinism: fixed seeds, sorted joins, UTC timestamps, no wall-clock in features.

## Conventions

- Smallest working diff. No speculative abstractions, no config for values that never
  change, no frameworks. Delete dead code instead of preserving it "for later".
- Timestamps: `pandas` UTC-aware datetimes end to end.
- Prefer flat modules in `src/` (`fetch.py`, `features.py`, `evaluate.py`, ...) over
  packages with one class each.
- Comments explain *why*, never *what*. Mark deliberate ceilings with
  `ponytail: <ceiling>; upgrade path: <what to add>`.

## Definition of done

**Input:**

```json
  "command": "env | grep -iE 'alert|alarm|token' | sed 's/=.*/=<set>/'; python3 --version; python3 -c \"import pandas, numpy; print(pandas.__version__, numpy.__version__)\" 2>&1 | tail -2"
}
```

# AGENTS.md

Operating agreement for agents working in this repo. Read this first, then read `GOAL.md`.

## Goal

Time series analysis + forecasting of air raid alerts in Ukraine, evaluated on the latest
data with zero data leakage. Ship a working, reproducible pipeline and a short write-up.

## Current layout

```
GOAL.md          # problem, data sources, evaluation rules
AGENTS.md        # this file
opencode.json    # agent tooling config
data/raw/        # fetched data + manifest.json (gitignored)
data/processed/  # derived features (gitignored)
src/             # pipeline code
notebooks/       # exploration only; nothing final lives here
reports/         # figures + findings.md
tests/           # self-checks, runnable via `python -m pytest -q`
```

Missing directories are created when first needed — don't pre-scaffold.

## Commands

- Python: system `python3` (3.14). Use `uv` if installing packages; pin them in
  `pyproject.toml` when (and only when) a dependency is actually needed. Start with
  stdlib + pandas/numpy; add statsmodels/scikit-learn/lightgbm only if used.
- Tests: `python -m pytest -q`. One self-check per non-trivial module is enough.
- Data fetch scripts must be idempotent and safe to re-run: they skip or upsert, and
  always refresh `data/raw/manifest.json`.

## Hard rules (violating these fails the project)

1. **No leakage.** Nothing trained on or used as input may exist after the forecast origin.
   Scalers/encoders/imputers are fit on the training window only. Labels may use the
   future; features may not.
2. **Holdout is the latest period**, time-ordered, untouched until final evaluation.
   No random shuffling of time series. Use rolling-origin backtests for tuning.
3. **Never commit** `data/raw/`, `data/processed/`, tokens, or credentials. Tokens come
   from env vars (`ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN`, `UKRAINE_ALARM_TOKEN`). `.gitignore` enforces this.
4. **Data provenance:** every dataset you use gets a manifest entry (source, query,
   fetch time, row count, timestamp range). Claims in `reports/findings.md` cite it.
5. **Baselines first.** Seasonal-naive / hour-of-week profile before any fancy model,
   and always compared against in the report.
6. Determinism: fixed seeds, sorted joins, UTC timestamps, no wall-clock in features.

## Conventions

- Smallest working diff. No speculative abstractions, no config for values that never
  change, no frameworks. Delete dead code instead of preserving it "for later".
- Timestamps: `pandas` UTC-aware datetimes end to end.
- Prefer flat modules in `src/` (`fetch.py`, `features.py`, `evaluate.py`, ...) over
  packages with one class each.
- Comments explain *why*, never *what*. Mark deliberate ceilings with
  `ponytail: <ceiling>; upgrade path: <what to add>`.

## Definition of done

- `python -m pytest -q` passes.
- Reproducible run: `python -m src.fetch && python -m src.evaluate` produces the numbers
  in `reports/findings.md` (figures in `reports/figures/`).
- `reports/findings.md` states: data window and source, forecast setup and origins,
  metrics vs. baselines on the holdout, leakage checklist (explicit "what is known at
  forecast time" table), and honest limitations.

## [GitHub - rudakovevgenij/ukraine-air-raid-analysis: A time-series analysis of air raid alerts in Ukraine (2022-2026), focusing on true tactical durations and geographic burden. · GitHub](https://github.com/rudakovevgenij/ukraine-air-raid-analysis)

rudakovevgenij/ukraine-air-raid-analysis
* Page: GitHub repository
* URL: https://github.com/rudakovevgenij/ukraine-air-raid-analysis
* Description: A time-series analysis of air raid alerts in Ukraine (2022-2026), focusing on true tactical durations and geographic burden. - rudakovevgenij/ukraine-air-raid-analysis
* Stars: 0
* Forks: 0
* Default branch: main
Ukraine Air Raid Alerts Analysis (2022-2026)
This project analyzes data on air raid alerts in Ukraine to understand the true impact of the alarms: how often they happen, what time of day they usually start, and which regions are hit the hardest.
How the Data Was Cleaned
If we counted every row in the database, it would falsely look like five separate attacks. To fix this, the code merges any overlapping alarms in the same region into one single, continuous event.
Filtering Out Alerts Over 24 Hours: Frontline regions occasionally have alerts that stay on for days or weeks at a time (usually due to constant artillery threats or system glitches). To make sure we are only looking at standard air raids (like drones and missiles), I filtered out any individual alert that lasted longer than 24 hours.
Fixing Timezones: The raw data logs were saved in UTC time. I converted all timestamps to Kyiv local time so that our "Hour of the Day" analysis accurately reflects what time it actually is for the people on the ground.
The Charts
1. Monthly Timeline
This chart tracks the total number of hours regions spent under alert each month. It shows a steady climb from late 2023 onward, reflecting the shift toward mass drone attacks, which take much longer to cross the country than fast-moving missiles.
2. Time of Day Pattern
This chart breaks down the exact hour alerts are triggered.
By converting the data to local time, it clearly shows that the most dangerous time of day is 21:00 (9:00 PM), as threats are launched to cross into the country under the cover of darkness.
3. Hardest-Hit Regions
This is a ranking of regions based on their total cumulative hours under standard air raid alerts.
Note on missing data: Luhansk Oblast and Crimea are deliberately excluded from this main chart. Because they have been under a permanent, uninterrupted alert status since the start of the full-scale invasion (over 38,000 hours), including them would completely break the visual scale for the rest of the country.
They are noted in a separate warning box on the dashboard.
4. Alert Duration Histogram
This chart shows how long a typical alert actually lasts. The data is grouped into 15-minute buckets. While some alerts last much longer, the visual chart is capped at 5 hours (300 minutes) to keep the layout readable.
This 5-hour window successfully captures over 92% of all historical alerts.
How to Run This Project
Clone the repository to your computer.
Make sure clean_air_raid_data.csv is in the same folder.
Open 03_Final_Report_Air_Raid_Analysis.ipynb and run all cells. (Requires pandas, matplotlib, and seaborn).

README.md
Ukraine Air Raid Alert Patterns
An interactive Streamlit app that visualizes when and where air raid alerts occur across Ukrainian oblasts — based on real historical data from 2022 to the present.
Built for KSE Agentic AI Summer School 2026 — Stage 2.
What the app does
The app answers one question: at what time of day, and in which region, are air raid alerts most likely?
It shows three views:
* Main — a choropleth map of Ukraine colored by alert probability at the selected hour, plus an hourly trend chart and top-risk regions bar chart
* Heatmap — a full 25-oblast x 24-hour probability matrix
* Detailed Statistics — per-oblast alert duration analysis and a sortable summary table
* Crimea is shown in gray — it is Temporarily Occupied Territory with no Ukrainian alert data available.
Data sources
* Source | Description
* Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset | Oblast-level air raid alert records with start/end timestamps (UTC), from March 2022
* Natural Earth ADM1 | Ukraine administrative boundaries (oblast level)
  All timestamps are converted to Kyiv local time (Europe/Kiev, UTC+2/+3).
Methodology
For each (oblast, hour) pair:
P(alert at hour h in oblast r) = days with at least one alert at hour h / total days in selected period
This is an empirical frequency — not a forecast. The result shows "on what fraction of days did an alert occur at this time in this region?"
Key findings
* Night hours (01:00-05:00) show the highest average alert probability across all regions
* Eastern oblasts (Kharkiv, Donetsk, Luhansk, Sumy) show consistently higher probabilities at all hours
* Western oblasts (Zakarpattia, Chernivtsi, Ivano-Frankivsk) show significantly lower and more time-concentrated risk
File structure
ukraine-alerts/
├── app.py                     # Main Streamlit application
├── ukraine_oblasts.geojson    # Pre-generated Ukraine oblast boundaries
├── requirements.txt
└── README.md

## [GitHub - mrartur343/predict-alerts-api: API for predicting ...](https://github.com/mrartur343/predict-alerts-api)

GitHub - mrartur343/predict-alerts-api: API for predicting ...
Overview This project provides a set of tools for predicting air raid alerts in Ukraine. It uses historical data to train a GBM (Gradient Boosting Model) that can forecast the likelihood of future air raid alerts. The system is designed to be interactive, allowing users to specify parameters for both training and prediction processes.

## [Predictive Analytics of Air Alerts in the Russian-Ukrainian War](https://arxiv.org/html/2411.14625v1)

At the same time, there are many datasets with historical data about air alerts. Our experience of air alerts intuitively shows that there is a geospatial pattern in emerging alerts in different regions of Ukraine.
For our study, we took historical data from [ 1 ] . Here we present some results about exploratory data analysis. Figure 1 shows the heatmap for the total duration of air alerts in Ukrainian regions (oblasts) in minutes. For Luhansk region, the dataset contains only short time historical data.
Figure 2 shows a heatmap for regions’ total duration of air alerts which occurred simultaneously with Kharkiv oblast. One can see that the alerts from neighbouring regions have higher correlation with air alerts in Kharkiv oblast if compared to more distant Western regions. Figure 3 shows the daily median for alert durations by regions in minutes.
Figure 4 shows the boxplots for the duration of alerts by regions in minutes. Figure 5 shows the boxplots for the total duration of air alerts per day by regions in minutes. For further analysis, we created an alert_ts dataframe with the time series index from Mar 25 2022 to Nov 6 2024 with the granularity of 1 minute.
When the air alert starts in a region, then this feature is equal to the current duration of the alert. When the alert in the region is over, then the feature will have the value 0. For predictive analytics study, we chose the following regions: Lvivska oblast, Vinnytska oblast, Kyivska oblast, Kharkivska oblast .

## 🚨 About Datasets

There are two sources of alerts: official
and unofficial (collected by volunteers from [eTryvoga](https://app.etryvoga.com) channel).

Both datasets will be updated daily. All times are in UTC.

### Official dataset

You may see `official_data_en.csv` (🇬🇧) and `official_data_uk.csv` (🇺🇦) files.
They're identical but in different language.

Official dataset contains information from 15th of March 2022 – it's the first day when siren record occurs.
Since December, 2025th there is mostly air raid alerts on raion (district) level ([see post in the media](https://texty.org.ua/fragments/116501/v-ukrayini-zaprovadzhuyetsya-novyj-pidxid-do-oholoshennya-povitryanyx-tryvoh-svyrydenko/)), before sirens were applied to the whole oblast (region). You may find them on [wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raions_of_Ukraine).
On some .geojson files they're Subnational Administrative Boundaries (Admin 2: 139 Raion (District))

### Volunteer datasets

Data by volunteers are stored in `volunteer_data_uk.csv` (🇺🇦) and `volunteer_data_en.csv` (🇬🇧).

It contains more data (starts from 25th of February – second day of war!) and only on oblast (region) level.

If there are no messages about the end of the sirens,
you may see them with `naive=True` and `finished_at = started_at + 30 minutes`.

Thanks to [eTryvoga](https://app.etryvoga.com) channel for this data.

## 🤔 Good to Know

There are two permanent sirens:

1. In **Luhansk region** from April 4 at 04:45 PM (UTC+00) or April 4 at 07:45 PM (local time)
2. In **Crimea** from December 10 at 10:22 PM (UTC+00) or December 11 at 12:22 AM (local time)

They are not listed in datasets, so you may want to process them manually.

Also maybe a "missile/drone events" dataset... There's the "Air Force of Ukraine" telegram reports — hard. Open-Meteo is free without key, has archive up to ~5 days ago and forecast. Weather is genuinely known at forecast time for short horizons (forecast) — careful: using *observed* weather at time t for a forecast made at t-24h would be leakage-ish; but for horizon ≤ 24h, use forecast weather or lag. - Horizon: 24h ahead (daily), issued at a fixed origin (e.g. 00:00 UTC or 18:00 Kyiv), predicting (a) alert count per oblast per day, (b) alert-start events per hour, (c) total alert-minutes per day, (d) duration of the next alert.
- Targets: 3 tasks per GOAL: starts (when), counts (how many), duration (how long).

- Model 1 (interpretable): Poisson/negative-binomial GLM for daily counts with hour-of-week + trend... statsmodels.
- Model 2 (real): gradient boosting on lag features — LightGBM or sklearn HistGradientBoostingRegressor (sklearn is a big dep but fine). Actually LightGBM handles Poisson objective + count data well.
- Model 3: event-time/survival for next-start: could be simple empirical hazard by hour-of-week, or Cox... overkill. Keep to a discrete-time hazard model (logistic regression on time-since-last-alert features) — interpretable and cheap (sklearn LogisticRegression or statsmodels).

Given "lazy senior dev", keep the model set small: 2 models + 2 baselines.

Duration: log-duration MAE + empirical coverage → quantile regression (LightGBM quantile or conformal intervals). Conformal prediction on residuals is cheap and gives coverage guarantees — nice touch for "empirical coverage" metric. Actually the GOAL suggests "MAE on log-duration + empirical coverage" — so predict duration with a point model and add conformal intervals from calibration window.

## Data reality check (done before proposing anything)

**The official source has a structural break exactly where our holdout lives.** `Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset` (auto-updated by CI, last commit today) ships two datasets:

| dataset | window | rows | granularity | fresh? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `official_data_en.csv` | 2022-03-15 → **2026-09-07** | 299k | oblast **until Nov 2025, then raion/hromada** | stale ~5 weeks |
| `volunteer_data_en.csv` (eTryvoga) | 2022-02-25 → **2026-10-11 04:36** | 116k | oblast + Kyiv City, constant | hours old |

Since Dec 2025 the official feed is raion-level (48 oblast-rows/month vs 14k raion-rows). Our holdout is the *latest* period, so the official data forces us to reconstruct oblast alerts from districts in the eval window while training on whole-oblast alerts — a regime artifact masquerading as signal. → **Primary series: the volunteer oblast-level dataset**; official only as an optional cross-check.

Other properties I measured (they shape cleaning):
- 25 units: 23 oblasts + Kyiv City + 3 stray Luhansk rows (Luhansk/Crimea have permanent sirens and are deliberately absent). No overlapping consecutive alerts per region — no merging needed.
- 5% of rows are `naive=True` (end message never arrived; end = start + 30 min). Exactly 50% of all alerts are ≤30 min → these are **right-censored durations**, must not be trained as true durations.
- Durations: p50=30 min, p90=104, p99=266, max 7.4 days (front-line "permanent" alerts). Volume doubled 2022→2026 (drone regime) → non-stationary, don't let old years dominate the profile.

Free external data, verified reachable, no keys: **Open-Meteo** (hourly weather + daily sunrise/sunset per region centroid, one call covers the whole window), plus stdlib `zoneinfo` for Europe/Kyiv calendar.

## Vision

**Forecast setup.** One coherent setup instead of three ad-hoc ones: origin = 00:00 Kyiv daily, horizon = next 24 h, unit = region × hour. Every row of every eval gets an explicit `origin`; the feature builder takes `origin` as an argument and asserts nothing it reads has ts ≥ origin (that assert *is* the leakage test).

Three targets, all derived from the same frame:
1. **Counts** — alert starts per region per day (+ alert-minutes/day as secondary). Poisson-ish.
2. **Start timing** — hourly binary "≥1 alert starts in this hour"; eval as P/R/F1 with ±1 h tolerance on matched hours.
3. **Duration** — conditional on a start occurring, predict log-duration from what's known at start time (region, local hour/dow, time since last alert, trailing alert rate, weather lags, daylight). MAE on log-duration + **split-conformal** intervals on the tune window for the coverage metric.

**Feature families (all knowable at origin):** calendar (dow/hour/season/UA holidays) · lags (starts in past 1/6/24/72 h, alert-minutes past 24 h, time since last alert, count of regions alerting nationally in past 3 h — mass attacks are nationwide and that's a strong leading signal) · trailing 28-day rate per region · weather *lags* only + sunrise/sunset (known years ahead, and drone waves are night-bound).

**Leakage traps I'll explicitly handle:** weather at the target hour is future → use lags only (upgrade path: Open-Meteo *forecast* API at origin); climatology profiles and any scaling fit on train window only; `naive` rows censored in the duration label.

**Models — 2 baselines, 2 models, one dependency stack (pandas/numpy/scikit-learn/matplotlib):**
- Baseline A: hour-of-week empirical profile per region (the "alerts happen at the usual times" bar).
- Baseline B: seasonal naive (same hour last week).
- Model 1 (interpretable): linear PoissonRegressor (counts) + LogisticRegression (hourly starts) — coefficients readable in the report.
- Model 2 (real): HistGradientBoosting — `loss="poisson"` for counts, `loss="quantile"` + conformal for duration.

**Evaluation:** split per GOAL — train ≤ T−28d, tune T−28d…T−14d, test = last 14 d (2026-09-27 → 2026-10-11 here), untouched. ≥3 rolling-origin folds for model/HP choice inside the train region. Metrics per region *and* macro-averaged; calibration = predicted vs observed alert-time share per oblast. Findings must include the "known at forecast time" table.

**Modules (flat, per AGENTS):** `src/fetch.py` (snapshot download + manifest with git SHA, row counts, ts range) · `src/features.py` (clean + origin-guarded matrix builder) · `src/models.py` · `src/evaluate.py` (splits, backtests, metrics, figures) · `src/eda.py` (seasonality/clustering/gaps/regime figures) · one test each.

Four forks I want your call on before I write this up as the final plan:

Let me draft the final plan concisely.

Locked in: volunteer snapshot primary, daily 00:00 Kyiv origin / 24 h horizon, scikit-learn only, weather-lags + daylight + UA holidays. Here's the final plan.

## Plan

### 1. Data (`src/fetch.py`)
- Download `datasets/volunteer_data_en.csv` from `Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset` raw URL → `data/raw/`. Idempotent: re-run overwrites, always refreshes `data/raw/manifest.json` with source URL, upstream git SHA (GitHub API), fetch time, row count, min/max ts, schema hash. Also pulls Open-Meteo per region centroid (hourly weather 2022-02-25→now + daily sunrise/sunset) → `data/raw/weather.csv`, own manifest entry.
- `python -m src.fetch` is the only network entry point.

### 2. Clean + features (`src/features.py`)
- 24 units (23 oblasts + Kyiv City); drop the 3 stray Luhansk rows with a comment. Index = UTC hourly per region; calendar math in Europe/Kyiv via `zoneinfo`.
- `naive=True` rows keep their start event but get `duration_censored=True` → excluded from duration labels (report their share). Alerts >24 h excluded from duration training with a `ponytail:` note (0.01% of rows).
- **Origin-guarded builder**: `build(origin, history) -> row` asserts `max(input ts) < origin`. This assert is the leakage test — the same call is used in training and eval, so there's no second code path to get wrong.
- Features: dow/hour/month/weekend/UA-holiday · starts in past 1/6/24/72 h, alert-minutes past 24 h, time since last alert · count of regions alerting nationally in past 3 h (mass-attack leading signal) · trailing 28-day rate · weather **lags only** (temp, cloud, wind, precip at t−1…t−24) · daylight length + minutes-to-sunset.
- Targets per origin: `count_starts(region, day)`, `alert_minutes(region, day)`, `start_flag(region, hour)`, `log_duration(next alert | it starts)`.

### 3. Regression (`src/models.py`)
- **Baselines first**: A) hour-of-week empirical profile per region, fit on train window only; B) seasonal naive = same hour last week. Everything below must beat these or the report says so.
- **Model 1 — interpretable**: `PoissonRegressor` (daily counts; log-link, coefficients expoundable per feature) + `LogisticRegression` (hourly start probability, L2, standardized on train-only stats).
- **Model 2 — boosted**: `HistGradientBoostingRegressor` — `loss="poisson"` for counts/minutes, `loss="quantile"` (q=0.1/0.5/0.9) for log-duration. Same feature matrix as Model 1, so the report compares features-vs-learner, not two data pipelines.
- Duration eval needs intervals → **split conformal**: fit on train, calibrate residuals on the tune window, wrap Model 2's median prediction. Gives the "empirical coverage" metric honestly with ~10 lines.

### 4. Evaluation (`src/evaluate.py`)
- Splits: train ≤ T−28d · tune T−28d…T−14d · **test = last 14 d, touched once, at the end**. Rolling-origin backtest (4 folds) inside train for HP selection only.
- Metrics: counts → MAE/RMSE vs seasonal naive, per region + macro · starts → P/R/F1 on hour matching within ±1 h · duration → MAE(log-duration) + coverage of conformal intervals · calibration → predicted vs observed alert-time share per oblast.
- Writes `reports/figures/*.png` (seasonality heatmap, hour-of-week profile, predicted-vs-observed, calibration) and the numbers tables for `findings.md`.
- `src/eda.py` separate: seasonality, clustering/gap distribution, regime shift 2022→2026 (volume doubled), per-oblast burden. Figures only, no numbers in `findings.md` sourced from it without citation.

### 5. Report (`reports/findings.md`)
Data window + manifest citations · forecast setup and origins · metrics vs both baselines on holdout · **"known at forecast time" table** (each feature: timestamp rule, why safe) · limitations (volunteer-sourced data, 5% censored ends, weather-as-lags not forecasts, oblast-level hides raion regime change after Dec 2025, Luhansk/Crimea absent).

### 6. Tests (`tests/`, `python -m pytest -q`)
Four, one per module: fetch manifest fields present · **`build()` raises when handed post-origin data** · baseline beats a trivial constant on train · conformal interval coverage is in [0.5, 1.0] on a toy series.

Deps: `pandas numpy scikit-learn matplotlib pytest` pinned in `pyproject.toml`, installed with `uv`.

**Deliberate ceilings** (marked `ponytail:` in code): snapshot instead of API (upgrade path: `alerts.in.ua` client when a token exists) · weather lags instead of forecast-at-origin (upgrade path: Open-Meteo forecast API) · no Telegram/missile-event signals (upgrade path: DSNS channel export with post timestamps).

Order of build: `fetch` → `features` (+leakage test) → `eda` → `models` → `evaluate` → `findings.md`. Say go (or adjust anything above) — and since implementing means editing files, you'll need to switch me out of plan mode first.

1: # Air Raid Alerts Ukraine — Time Series Analysis
2: 
3: Mini pet-project (KSE Agentic AI, stage 2): time series analysis and forecasting of air
4: raid alerts in Ukraine — a real-world defense problem.
5: 
6: ## Problem
7: 
8: Air raid sirens in Ukraine follow strong temporal structure: daily/weekly seasonality,
9: clustering during mass attacks, quiet stretches between them. The task is to turn the
10: alert history into useful forecasts: **when alerts are likely to start, how many to expect,
11: and how long the next alert will last**.
12: 
13: Concretely, build a pipeline that:
14: 
15: 1. **Ingests** the most recent alert history available (per oblast and/or nationwide).
16: 2. **Explores** it: seasonality, trends, clustering, gaps, changes in alert regimes.
17: 3. **Forecasts** the latest period with at least one interpretable baseline
18:    (e.g. historical hour-of-week profile) and at least one real model
19:    (e.g. gradient boosting on lag features, or a count/event-time model).
20: 4. **Evaluates** honestly on the most recent data (see below) and writes up findings.
21: 
22: ## Data
23: 
24: Use the most recent data you can get at run time. Candidates (in order of preference):
25: 
26: - **`alerts.in.ua` API** — historical alerts per oblast (token via `ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN`).
27: - **`api.ukrainealarm.com`** — Ukraine Alarm API (token via `UKRAINE_ALARM_TOKEN`).
28: - **Public snapshots** (fallback / reproducibility): `vadimkin/ukraine-air-raid-sirens-dataset`
29:   (GitHub), Kaggle / Hugging Face mirrors of alert histories.
30: 
31: Rules:
32: 
33: - API tokens live in env vars only — never in code, data, or git.
34: - Raw data lands in `data/raw/` and is **never committed**. Record the exact
35:   `data/raw/manifest.json`: source, query, fetch time, row counts, min/max timestamps.
36: - Prefer per-oblast alerts at alert-start/stop granularity. Downstream, aggregate freely
37:   (e.g. hourly alert-seconds per oblast).
38: 
39: Optional extensions (nice-to-have, not required): text/media signals that existed at
40: forecast time — official channel posts (Telegram `@air_alert_ua` / DSNS), weather,
41: missile/drone event reports. These must obey the same timestamp cutoff as everything else.
42: 
43: ## Evaluation & no leakage
44: 
45: **Leakage is a hard failure, not a warning.** Everything a forecast uses must be knowable
46: at the moment the forecast is made.
47: 
48: - Choose an explicit **forecast origin** for every evaluation row. Any feature, label,
49:   scaler, encoder, or hyperparameter input with a timestamp after the origin is a leak.
50: - Fit preprocessing (scalers, target encoders, imputers) on the training window only.
51: - **Holdout = the latest period**, never shuffled and never trained on. Suggested split:
52:   train on everything up to `T-28 days`, tune on `T-28d..T-14d`, test on the last 14 days.
53:   If the data ends earlier than today, "latest period" means the latest period *in the data*.
54: - Report baselines alongside the model. If the model can't beat "alerts happen at the usual
55:   times", say so.
56: - Suggested metrics (pick per task, state why):
57:   - alert count per day/oblast: MAE / RMSE vs. seasonal naive;
58:   - alert start events: precision/recall/F1 within a tolerance window (±15–30 min);
59:   - duration: MAE on log-duration + empirical coverage;
60:   - calibration: predicted vs. observed alert-time share per oblast.
61: - Backtesting: rolling-origin evaluation (≥ 3 folds), never random K-fold on time.
62: 
63: ## Status
64: 
65: Scaffolding only — the pipeline, models, and write-up are to be built in this repo.
66: See `AGENTS.md` for the working agreement and current layout.

72:   3. **duration** — log-duration of the next alert, modelled *conditional on a start*
73:      (features known at start time), evaluated with MAE + split-conformal coverage.
74: - Feature families (all knowable at origin): calendar (dow/hour/month/season/weekend/UA
75:   holidays) · lags (starts in past 1/6/24/72 h, alert-minutes past 24 h, time since last
76:   alert) · count of regions alerting nationally in the past 3 h (mass attacks are nationwide,
77:   a strong leading signal) · trailing 28-day rate per region · weather lags · daylight.
78: - Stack: **pandas / numpy / scikit-learn / matplotlib** only.
79: 
80: ## Model ladder
81: 
82: Baselines first, then two models that share one feature matrix (so the report compares
83: features vs learner, not two data pipelines):
84: 
85: - **Baseline A** — hour-of-week empirical profile per region, fit on the train window only.
86: - **Baseline B** — seasonal naive (same hour last week).
87: - **Model 1 (interpretable)** — `PoissonRegressor` for daily counts
88:   (log-link, coefficients expoundable) + `LogisticRegression` for hourly start probability.
89: - **Model 2 (real)** — `HistGradientBoostingRegressor`: `loss="poisson"` for counts/minutes,
90:   `loss="quantile"` (0.1/0.5/0.9) for log-duration.
91: - Duration intervals via **split conformal**: fit on train, calibrate residuals on the tune
92:   window, wrap the median prediction — ~10 lines, honest coverage numbers.
93: 
94: ## Evaluation & no leakage
95: 
96: **Leakage is a hard failure, not a warning.** Everything a forecast uses must be knowable
97: at the moment the forecast is made.
98: 
99: - Choose an explicit **forecast origin** for every evaluation row. Any feature, label,
100:   scaler, encoder, or hyperparameter input with a timestamp after the origin is a leak.
101: - Fit preprocessing (scalers, target encoders, imputers) on the training window only.
102: - **Holdout = the latest period**, never shuffled and never trained on. Suggested split:
103:   train on everything up to `T-28 days`, tune on `T-28d..T-14d`, test on the last 14 days.
104:   If the data ends earlier than today, "latest period" means the latest period *in the data*.
105: - Report baselines alongside the model. If the model can't beat "alerts happen at the usual
106:   times", say so.
107: - Suggested metrics (pick per task, state why):
108:   - alert count per day/oblast: MAE / RMSE vs. seasonal naive;
109:   - alert start events: precision/recall/F1 within a tolerance window (±15–30 min);
110:   - duration: MAE on log-duration + empirical coverage;
111:   - calibration: predicted vs. observed alert-time share per oblast.
112: - Backtesting: rolling-origin evaluation (≥ 3 folds), never random K-fold on time.
113:   Folds are used for model/hyperparameter choice only; the last 14 days are touched once.
114: 
115: ## Build plan
116: 
117: Flat modules (per `AGENTS.md`), built in this order:
118: 
119: 1. `src/fetch.py` — snapshot + Open-Meteo download, idempotent, writes `manifest.json`.
120: 2. `src/features.py` — clean (drop stray Luhansk rows, flag `naive` rows as censored, drop
121:    alerts >24 h from duration labels) and the origin-guarded feature/target builder.
122: 3. `src/eda.py` — seasonality, clustering/gaps, regime shift 2022→2026, per-oblast burden.
123: 4. `src/models.py` — baselines + the two models.
124: 5. `src/evaluate.py` — splits, rolling-origin backtest, metrics, conformal calibration,
125:    figures to `reports/figures/`, numbers for `findings.md`.
126: 6. `reports/findings.md` — data window + manifest citations, forecast setup and origins,
127:    metrics vs baselines on the holdout, an explicit **"known at forecast time"** table
128:    (each feature: timestamp rule, why safe), and honest limitations (volunteer-sourced data,
129:    censored ends, weather-as-lags, oblast granularity hides the post-Dec-2025 raion regime,
130:    Luhansk/Crimea absent).
131: 
132: Remaining ceilings, marked in code: snapshot instead of API; weather lags instead of
133: forecast-at-origin; no Telegram / missile-event signals.
134: 
135: ## Status
136: 
137: Planned and agreed — implementation starts at `src/fetch.py`. Definition of done and
138: working agreement live in `AGENTS.md`.

## Plan

**Phase 0 — env bootstrap** (me, serial)
Install `uv` per AGENTS.md (fallback: plain `venv`+`pip`), create `pyproject.toml` pinning exactly `pandas numpy scikit-learn matplotlib` + `pytest` (dev), smoke-import all four. Risk: 3.14 wheel availability for sklearn; fallback is `uv python install 3.13`.

**Phase 1 — `src/fetch.py`** (me, serial — single network writer, shared dependency for everything)
Streaming download of `volunteer_data_en.csv` → `data/raw/`, plus Open-Meteo hourly weather + daylight per oblast coords. Idempotent (skip/upsert by content hash), always writes `data/raw/manifest.json`: upstream SHA, fetch time, row counts, min/max timestamps, file sha256. One test: re-run is a no-op and manifest refreshes.

**Phase 2 — `src/features.py`** (one `general` subagent; critical path — it *is* the contract)
Cleaning rules from GOAL (drop 3 Luhansk strays, flag `naive=True` as censored, drop >24 h alerts from duration labels only), then the origin-guarded builder: `build_features(df, origin)` with the `assert all(feature_ts < origin)` gate as the *only* path train and eval share. Targets: daily counts/alert-minutes, hourly start binary, log-duration conditional on start. `tests/test_features.py` includes a deliberate post-origin row → assert must fire.

**Phase 3 — parallel, 2 `general` subagents** (both import Phase 2's contract)
| Agent | Task | Output |
|---|---|---|
| A | `src/eda.py` | seasonality, clustering/gaps, 2022→2026 regime shift, per-oblast burden; figures → `reports/figures/` |
| B | `src/models.py` | Baseline A (hour-of-week profile), Baseline B (seasonal naive), Model 1 (PoissonRegressor + LogisticRegression), Model 2 (HistGB poisson/quantile) — all on the one shared feature matrix |

Each leaves one assert-based test.

**Phase 4 — `src/evaluate.py`** (me, serial — depends on both of Phase 3)
Split: train ≤ T−28 d, tune T−28..T−14, test last 14 d, rolling-origin ≥3 folds for choice only. Metrics: MAE vs seasonal naive (counts), precision/recall/F1 ±15–30 min (starts), log-MAE + split-conformal coverage (duration), calibration plot. Holdout touched once, at the end.

**Phase 5 — `reports/findings.md`** (me; can be drafted by a subagent once numbers land)
Manifest citations, setup/origins, metrics vs baselines, "known at forecast time" table, limitations. Then DoD gate: `python -m pytest -q`, `python -m src.fetch && python -m src.evaluate` reproduces the numbers.

Parallelism summary: fetch → features are serial (real data + shared contract), then EDA ∥ models, evaluate/findings serial. I deliberately don't parallelize cleaning across agents — duplicated cleaning logic is exactly the kind of drift that produces leakage bugs.

| 3 | `src/eda.py` ∥ `src/models.py` + tests: seasonality/clustering/regime-shift/burden figures ∥ Baseline A/B + Poisson/Logistic + HistGB on the shared matrix | 2× `general` subagents | ✅ |
| 4 | `src/evaluate.py`: train ≤ T−28d / tune T−28..T−14 / test last 14d, rolling-origin ≥3 folds, metrics (MAE vs seasonal-naive, P/R/F1 ±15–30 min, log-MAE + conformal coverage, calibration), figures | me | — |
| 5 | `reports/findings.md` + DoD gate: `python -m pytest -q`, `python -m src.fetch && python -m src.evaluate` reproduces numbers | me (draft via subagent optional) | — |

Only Phase 3 is parallel — `features.py` defines the shared data contract, and duplicating cleaning across agents is exactly how leakage bugs creep in. Everything else sits on a dependency chain.

Design for fetch.py:

For download: 
```python
def download(url, dest: Path) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    tmp = dest.with_suffix(dest.suffix + ".tmp")
    tmp.write_bytes(get(url))
    new = sha256(tmp)
    if dest.exists() and sha256(dest) == new:
        tmp.unlink(); return new, False
    tmp.replace(dest); return new, True
```
This downloads every time (not skipping), but "skip" the write. Idempotent. Good and simple.

29:     "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset"
30:     "/main/datasets/volunteer_data_en.csv"
31: )
32: UPSTREAM_API = (
33:     "https://api.github.com/repos/Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset/commits/main"
34: )
35: WEATHER_URL = (
36:     "https://archive-api.open-meteo.com/v1/archive?latitude={lat}&longitude={lon}"
37:     "&start_date={start}&end_date={end}"
38:     "&hourly=temperature_2m,cloud_cover,wind_speed_10m,precipitation"
39:     "&daily=sunrise,sunset&timezone=UTC"
40: )
41: START_DATE = "2022-02-25"
42: 
43: # Oblast centroids, keyed by the exact region label in the snapshot (Luhanska excluded:
44: # 3 stray rows, no usable series).
45: REGIONS = {
46:     "Cherkaska oblast": (49.44, 32.06),
47:     "Chernihivska oblast": (51.50, 31.29),
48:     "Chernivetska oblast": (48.29, 25.94),
49:     "Dnipropetrovska oblast": (48.46, 35.05),
50:     "Donetska oblast": (48.02, 37.80),
51:     "Ivano-Frankivska oblast": (48.92, 24.71),
52:     "Kharkivska oblast": (49.99, 36.23),
53:     "Khersonska oblast": (46.64, 32.61),
54:     "Khmelnytska oblast": (49.42, 26.99),
55:     "Kirovohradska oblast": (48.51, 32.26),
56:     "Kyiv City": (50.45, 30.52),
57:     "Kyivska oblast": (49.80, 30.11),
58:     "Lvivska oblast": (49.84, 24.03),
59:     "Mykolaivska oblast": (46.97, 32.00),
60:     "Odeska oblast": (46.48, 30.73),
61:     "Poltavska oblast": (49.59, 34.55),
62:     "Rivnenska oblast": (50.62, 26.25),
63:     "Sumska oblast": (50.91, 34.80),
64:     "Ternopilska oblast": (49.55, 25.60),
65:     "Vinnytska oblast": (49.23, 28.47),
66:     "Volynska oblast": (50.75, 25.34),
67:     "Zakarpatska oblast": (48.62, 22.29),
68:     "Zaporizka oblast": (47.84, 35.14),
69:     "Zhytomyrska oblast": (50.25, 28.66),
70: }
71: 
72: WEATHER_COLUMNS = [
73:     "time",
74:     "temperature_2m",
75:     "cloud_cover",
76:     "wind_speed_10m",
77:     "precipitation",
78:     "sunrise",
79:     "sunset",
80: ]
81: 
82: 
83: def sha256(path: pathlib.Path) -> str:
84:     h = hashlib.sha256()
85:     with open(path, "rb") as f:
86:         for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(1 << 20), b""):
87:             h.update(chunk)
88:     return h.hexdigest()
89: 
90: 
91: def fetch_bytes(url: str, headers: dict | None = None, attempts: int = 4) -> bytes:
92:     """GET `url` into memory, retrying with backoff.
93: 
94:     Public endpoints (GitHub, Open-Meteo) reset connections under bursts, so a bare
95:     request intermittently dies mid-download.
96:     """
97:     for i in range(attempts):
98:         try:
99:             req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers=headers or {})
100:             with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=90) as resp:
101:                 return resp.read()
102:         except (urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError):
103:             if i == attempts - 1:
104:                 raise
105:             time.sleep(5 * 2**i)
106: 
107: 
108: def download(url: str, dest: pathlib.Path, headers: dict | None = None) -> str:
109:     """Fetch `url` into `dest`, returning its sha256. Leaves `dest` untouched when identical."""
110:     dest = pathlib.Path(dest)
111:     dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
112:     data = fetch_bytes(url, headers)
113:     new_sha = hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()
114:     if dest.exists() and sha256(dest) == new_sha:
115:         return new_sha
116:     fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=dest.parent, suffix=".tmp")
117:     os.close(fd)
118:     try:
119:         pathlib.Path(tmp).write_bytes(data)
120:         os.replace(tmp, dest)
121:     finally:
122:         if os.path.exists(tmp):
123:             os.unlink(tmp)
124:     return new_sha
125: 
126: 
127: def snapshot_summary(path: pathlib.Path) -> dict:
128:     """Row count and started_at range of the alert snapshot."""
129:     ts = pd.to_datetime(pd.read_csv(path, usecols=["started_at"])["started_at"], utc=True)
130:     return {
131:         "rows": int(len(ts)),
132:         "started_at_min": ts.min().isoformat(),
133:         "started_at_max": ts.max().isoformat(),
134:     }
135: 
136: 
137: def slugify(region: str) -> str:
138:     return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "_", region.lower()).strip("_")
139: 
140: 
141: def fetch_weather(lat: float, lon: float, start: str, end: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
142:     url = WEATHER_URL.format(lat=lat, lon=lon, start=start, end=end)
143:     payload = json.loads(fetch_bytes(url))
144:     hourly = payload["hourly"]
145:     df = pd.DataFrame(
146:         {
147:             "time": pd.to_datetime(hourly["time"], utc=True),
148:             "temperature_2m": hourly["temperature_2m"],
149:             "cloud_cover": hourly["cloud_cover"],
150:             "wind_speed_10m": hourly["wind_speed_10m"],
151:             "precipitation": hourly["precipitation"],
152:         }
153:     )
154:     daily = payload["daily"]
155:     by_day = pd.DataFrame(
156:         {
157:             "date": pd.to_datetime(daily["time"], utc=True).normalize(),
158:             "sunrise": pd.to_datetime(daily["sunrise"], utc=True),
159:             "sunset": pd.to_datetime(daily["sunset"], utc=True),
160:         }
161:     ).set_index("date")
162:     # Every hourly row inherits its own calendar day's sunrise/sunset (forward-filled across
163:     # any missing day at the range edges).
164:     day = df["time"].dt.normalize()
165:     df["sunrise"] = day.map(by_day["sunrise"]).ffill()
166:     df["sunset"] = day.map(by_day["sunset"]).ffill()
167:     return df[WEATHER_COLUMNS]
168: 
169: 
170: def weather_covers(path: pathlib.Path, end: str) -> bool:
171:     if not path.exists():
172:         return False
173:     last = pd.read_csv(path, usecols=["time"])["time"].iloc[-1]
174:     return pd.to_datetime(last, utc=True).date() >= date.fromisoformat(end)
175: 
176: 
177: def write_csv(df: pd.DataFrame, dest: pathlib.Path) -> None:
178:     dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
179:     fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=dest.parent, suffix=".tmp")
180:     os.close(fd)
181:     try:
182:         df.to_csv(tmp, index=False)
183:         os.replace(tmp, dest)
184:     finally:
185:         if os.path.exists(tmp):
186:             os.unlink(tmp)
187: 
188: 
189: def upstream_sha() -> str:
190:     headers = {"User-Agent": "kse-air-raid-alerts"}
191:     return json.loads(fetch_bytes(UPSTREAM_API, headers))["sha"]
192: 
193: 
194: def fetch_snapshot(fetched_at: str) -> dict:
195:     dest = RAW / "alerts_snapshot.csv"
196:     file_sha = download(SNAPSHOT_URL, dest)
197:     entry = {
198:         "source": "Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset (volunteer_data_en.csv)",
199:         "url": SNAPSHOT_URL,
200:         "query": "daily CI snapshot of per-region alert start/stop events, 2022-02-25 → now",
201:         "fetched_at": fetched_at,
202:         "upstream_sha": upstream_sha(),
203:         "path": str(dest),
204:         "sha256": file_sha,
205:     }
206:     entry.update(snapshot_summary(dest))
207:     return entry
208: 
209: 
210: def fetch_all_weather(start: str, end: str, fetched_at: str) -> dict:
211:     per_region = {}
212:     rows = 0
213:     t_min = t_max = None
214:     for region, (lat, lon) in REGIONS.items():
215:         path = WEATHER_DIR / f"{slugify(region)}.csv"
216:         if not weather_covers(path, end):
217:             write_csv(fetch_weather(lat, lon, start, end), path)
218:             time.sleep(1)  # spacing requests keeps us clear of Open-Meteo's burst limit
219:         ts = pd.to_datetime(pd.read_csv(path, usecols=["time"])["time"], utc=True)
220:         lo, hi = ts.min(), ts.max()
221:         rows += len(ts)
222:         t_min = lo if t_min is None else min(t_min, lo)
223:         t_max = hi if t_max is None else max(t_max, hi)
224:         per_region[region] = {
225:             "path": str(path),
226:             "rows": int(len(ts)),
227:             "time_min": lo.isoformat(),
228:             "time_max": hi.isoformat(),
229:         }
230:     return {
231:         "source": "Open-Meteo historical weather archive (ERA5)",
232:         "url_template": WEATHER_URL,
233:         "query": (
234:             f"hourly temperature_2m,cloud_cover,wind_speed_10m,precipitation + "
235:             f"daily sunrise,sunset at region centroids; {start} → {end}; timezone=UTC"
236:         ),
237:         "fetched_at": fetched_at,
238:         "start_date": start,
239:         "end_date": end,
240:         "region_count": len(REGIONS),
241:         "rows": rows,
242:         "time_min": t_min.isoformat(),
243:         "time_max": t_max.isoformat(),
244:         "regions": per_region,
245:     }
246: 
247: 
248: def main() -> None:
249:     RAW.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
250:     fetched_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
251:     end = datetime.now(timezone.utc).date().isoformat()
252:     manifest = {
253:         "generated_at": fetched_at,
254:         "alerts_snapshot": fetch_snapshot(fetched_at),
255:         "weather": fetch_all_weather(START_DATE, end, fetched_at),
256:     }
257:     (RAW / "manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n")
258:     snap = manifest["alerts_snapshot"]
259:     wx = manifest["weather"]
260:     print(f"alerts: {snap['rows']} rows {snap['started_at_min']} → {snap['started_at_max']}")
261:     print(f"weather: {wx['region_count']} regions, {wx['rows']} rows {wx['time_min']} → {wx['time_max']}")
262: 
263: 
264: if __name__ == "__main__":
265:     main()

Always run via `uv run python ...` / `uv run pytest -q` from the repo root. pandas 3.0 notes: string columns come back as `StringDtype`, `read_csv` parses the `naive` column of \"True\"/\"False\" straight to **bool**, text timestamps are NOT auto-parsed (use `pd.to_datetime(..., utc=True)`).\n\nINPUTS already on disk (from `src/fetch.py`, see `data/raw/manifest.json`):\n- `data/raw/alerts_snapshot.csv`: cols `region,started_at,finished_at,naive`. 115989 rows, UTC-aware time strings, `naive` bool. 25 regions incl. 3 stray `Luhanska oblast` rows. 14 rows have duration > 24 h. `naive=True` rows carry a synthetic end = start + 30 min (right-censored durations).\n- `data/raw/weather/<slug>.csv`, 24 regions: `time,temperature_2m,cloud_cover,wind_speed_10m,precipitation,sunrise,sunset`, all UTC-aware, hourly, 2022-02-25 → now. sunrise/sunset are that day's astronomical values (forward-filled per hour). Regions: Cherkaska oblast, Chernihivska oblast, Chernivetska oblast, Dnipropetrovska oblast, Donetska oblast, Ivano-Frankivska oblast, Kharkivska oblast, Khersonska oblast, Khmelnytska oblast, Kirovohradska oblast, Kyiv City, Kyivska oblast, Lvivska oblast, Mykolaivska oblast, Odeska oblast, Poltavska oblast, Rivnenska oblast, Sumska oblast, Ternopilska oblast, Vinnytska oblast, Volynska oblast, Zakarpatska oblast, Zaporizka oblast, Zhytomyrska oblast. Slug = region lowercased, non-alphanumerics → `_` (same as `src.fetch.slugify`).\n\nPUBLIC API (implement exactly):\n\n1. `KYIV = zoneinfo.ZoneInfo(\"Europe/Kyiv\")`\n2. `load_alerts(path=\"data/raw/alerts_snapshot.csv\") -> pd.DataFrame`\n   Columns: `region` (str), `started_at`, `finished_at` (UTC-aware), `naive` (bool), `duration_min` (float). Drops `Luhanska oblast`. `duration_min = (finished_at - started_at)` in minutes, set to **NaN** when the row is `naive` (right-censored) or `duration_min > 24*60` (label-only exclusion — never drop the start). Sort by `region, started_at`.\n3. `load_weather(dir=\"data/raw/weather\") -> pd.DataFrame`\n   Concatenate the per-region CSVs, add a `region` column, all times UTC-aware, sorted by `region, time`.\n4. `hourly_grid(alerts) -> pd.DataFrame`\n   Dense `region × hour` grid over the whole data span (hour = UTC floor-aligned hour). Columns: `region`, `hour` (UTC-aware), `minutes` (float: alert-seconds in that hour / 60, counting an alert's overlap with the hour; alerts running past data end or overlapping each other must still sum correctly, but note there are NO overlapping consecutive alerts in this data), `starts` (int: number of alerts whose `started_at` falls in that hour). This one grid is the source for every lag/label so the module stays fast.\n5. `daily_origin(day) -> pd.Timestamp` where `day` is a `str`/`datetime.date`: returns 00:00 Europe/Kyiv on that date as a **UTC-aware** Timestamp.\n6. `origins(start_day, end_day) -> list[pd.Timestamp]` — daily origins inclusive, via `daily_origin`.\n7. `build(origin, alerts=None, weather=None, grid=None) -> pd.DataFrame` — **the only feature path; train and eval both call it.**\n   Returns exactly `24 * n_regions` rows (24 consecutive UTC hours `origin + 0..23h` × each region), sorted by `region, hour`. `alerts`/`weather`/`grid` default to loading from disk (cache what you can, e.g. `functools.lru_cache`, so a rolling backtest over ~90 origins does not re-read 97 MB each time).\n   Columns: `origin`, `region`, `hour`, the feature columns below, and the three label columns.\n\n   FEATURE COLUMNS (every one must be derivable from a source whose timestamp is strictly < origin — see the guard):\n   - calendar: `hour_local` (0–23, Europe/Kyiv), `day_of_week` (0=Mon), `is_weekend`, `month`, `is_holiday` (fixed Ukrainian calendar; use month-day pairs: 01-01, 01-07, 03-08, 05-01, 05-09, 06-28, 08-24, 10-14, 12-25 — comment why these).\n   - lags from the grid, sliced to `hour < origin`: `starts_past_1h`, `starts_past_6h`, `starts_past_24h`, `starts_past_72h`, `alert_minutes_past_24h`, `trailing_28d_starts_per_day` (starts in the past 28 days / 28), `hours_since_last_alert` (NaN if the region never alerted before origin; cap at 24*90 to keep it bounded — comment the cap).\n   - national leading signal: `regions_alerting_past_3h` (number of distinct regions with `minutes > 0` in `[origin-3h, origin)`).\n   - weather lags, sliced to `time < origin`: `temp_mean_24h`, `cloud_mean_24h`, `wind_mean_24h`, `precip_sum_24h` (last 24 hourly rows before origin, per region).\n   - daylight (astronomically known years ahead, so NOT part of the guarded data path — say so in a comment): `minutes_to_sunset` = minutes from `hour` to that hour's `sunset` (negative = already after sunset), `is_night` = hour is before sunrise or after sunset, `day_length_min` = `sunset - sunrise` of that hour's local day. Look these up from the weather frame by `(region, date-of-hour)`; forward-fill within a region, and fill any remaining NaN with that region's median time-of-day for sunrise/sunset. For the horizons we evaluate the coverage is complete, so NaN should not normally occur.\n\n   LABEL COLUMNS (these MAY look at/after origin — that is the point; do not route them through the guard):\n   - `y_start` (int 0/1: an alert starts in that hour),\n   - `y_minutes` (float: alert-minutes in that hour; sum over a region's 24 rows = that day's alert-minutes),\n   - `y_dur_log` = `log1p(duration_min)` of the first alert starting in that hour, else **NaN** (no start, or censored/>24 h start). Sum of `y_start` over a region's 24 rows = that day's start count.\n\n   THE LEAKAGE GUARD (this is the whole point of the module): before using a source frame as feature input, slice it to timestamps `< origin` and then **assert** nothing at/after origin remains, e.g.\n   `feature_rows = grid[grid[\"hour\"] < origin]; assert (feature_rows[\"hour\"] < origin).all(), \"leak: feature row at/after origin\"`.\n   Make it a single small helper used by every feature family, and make the assert fire on a planted violation (see tests). Labels must never pass through it.\n\n8. `FEATURES: list[str]` — the ordered list of the feature column names above, for models to consume as `X = df[FEATURES]`.\n\nModule docstring should state it is the single origin-guarded feature builder shared by train and eval, and mark ceilings with `ponytail:` (e.g. weather as past-24h lags; upgrade path: read the Open-Meteo forecast at origin).\n\nTESTS in `tests/test_features.py` (offline, tiny synthetic fixtures — do not read `data/raw`, do not touch the network):\n- Guard fires: plant an alert exactly at `origin` in the synthetic frame and assert `build(...)` raises.\n- Lag correctness: one 30-minute alert starting 2 h before origin → in that region's rows `starts_past_24h == 1`, `alert_minutes_past_24h == 30`, `hours_since_last_alert == 2`.\n- Label correctness: with a duration-known alert starting at `origin + 3h`, `y_start` is 1 in that row, `y_dur_log == log1p(30)`, and `y_minutes == 30`; a censored (`naive=True`) start there leaves `y_dur_log` NaN but `y_start == 1`.\n\nTHEN verify for real against the actual data and report the numbers:\n- `uv run pytest -q` green.\n- Run a short script (put it in /tmp/opencode, NOT in the repo) that calls `build` for the last ~3 days of origins and prints: row count and region count, `FEATURES` list, dtype/NaN summary per feature, and total `y_start` / `y_dur_log` non-null counts. ## Files

## API (implemented exactly)
- `KYIV` = `ZoneInfo("Europe/Kyiv")`; `FEATURES` ordered list (20 names).
- `load_alerts(path=...) -> DataFrame[region, started_at, finished_at, naive, duration_min]` — drops `Luhanska oblast`; `duration_min` NaN when `naive` or `>1440`; sorted `region, started_at`. `lru_cache`d.
- `load_weather(dir=...) -> DataFrame` — per-region CSVs via `fetch.slugify`, UTC-aware, sorted. `lru_cache`d.
- `hourly_grid(alerts) -> DataFrame[region, hour, minutes, starts]` — dense grid, minutes split across hours (24+6 for a 10:36–11:06 alert).
- `load_grid(path=...)` — cached grid for the fast backtest path.
- `daily_origin(day)` / `origins(start_day, end_day)` — Kyiv midnight → UTC.
- `build(origin, alerts=None, weather=None, grid=None)` — 576 rows (24 regions × 24 h), sorted `region, hour`, cols `origin, region, hour, *FEATURES, y_start, y_minutes, y_dur_log`.
- Leakage guard: shared `_guard(frame, col, origin)` asserts the `< origin` slice holds nothing at/after origin; applied to grid/alerts/weather lags. Labels + daylight (astronomy) intentionally bypass it (commented).

1: """Origin-guarded feature + label builder for air-raid alert forecasting.
2: 
3: `build(origin)` is the single feature path shared by training and evaluation. It returns
4: one row per region × forecast hour (origin + 0..23 h, UTC) whose features are derived only

6: origin and therefore never pass through the leakage guard.

9: through one shared helper, `_guard`. Calendar and sunrise/sunset are deliberately outside
10: the guard: they are computable years ahead, so reading them for the horizon is not a leak.
11: 
12: ponytail: weather enters as past-24h lags; upgrade path: read the Open-Meteo forecast API
13: at origin. ponytail: fixed month-day UA holidays; upgrade path: Orthodox Easter/Trinity.
14: """

16: from __future__ import annotations
17:

20: import zoneinfo

30: DEFAULT_WEATHER = "data/raw/weather"

32: # Fixed Ukrainian public holidays as month-day (no Easter/Trinity, which move with the
33: # Orthodox calendar). Kept as month-day so it needs no yearly maintenance, and it is known
34: # years ahead — safe to attach to any forecast hour.
35: HOLIDAYS = {
36:     "01-01",  # New Year
37:     "01-07",  # Orthodox Christmas
38:     "03-08",  # International Women's Day
39:     "05-01",  # Labour Day

41:     "06-28",  # Constitution Day

44:     "12-25",  # Christmas

46: 
47: # Right-censored (naive) or implausibly long alerts keep their start event but drop out of
48: # the duration label; 24h is safely above any real single alert.
49: MAX_DURATION_MIN = 24 * 60
50: # Alerts can be silent for months (long quiet regimes); uncapped "hours since" would let
51: # the model extrapolate on an outlier. 90 days is well past the point where the signal
52: # saturates.
53: MAX_HOURS_SINCE = 24 * 90
54: 
55: SLUG_TO_REGION = {slugify(region): region for region in REGIONS}
56: 
57: FEATURES = [
58:     "hour_local",
59:     "day_of_week",
60:     "is_weekend",
61:     "month",
62:     "is_holiday",
63:     "starts_past_1h",
64:     "starts_past_6h",
65:     "starts_past_24h",

68:     "trailing_28d_starts_per_day",
69:     "hours_since_last_alert",

75:     "minutes_to_sunset",
76:     "is_night",
77:     "day_length_min",
78: ]
79: 
80: 
81: def _as_utc(ts) -> pd.Timestamp:
82:     ts = pd.Timestamp(ts)
83:     return ts.tz_localize("UTC") if ts.tzinfo is None else ts.tz_convert("UTC")
84: 
85: 
86: def _guard(frame: pd.DataFrame, col: str, origin: pd.Timestamp) -> pd.DataFrame:
87:     """Assert a feature slice holds nothing at/after `origin`. Labels never call this."""
88:     assert bool((frame[col] < origin).all()), "leak: feature row at/after origin"
89:     return frame
90: 
91: 
92: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=None)
93: def _load_alerts_cached(path: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
94:     df = pd.read_csv(path, usecols=["region", "started_at", "finished_at", "naive"])
95:     df["started_at"] = pd.to_datetime(df["started_at"], utc=True)
96:     df["finished_at"] = pd.to_datetime(df["finished_at"], utc=True)
97:     df = df[df["region"] != "Luhanska oblast"].copy()  # 3 stray rows, no usable series
98:     df["duration_min"] = (df["finished_at"] - df["started_at"]).dt.total_seconds() / 60.0
99:     censored = df["naive"] | (df["duration_min"] > MAX_DURATION_MIN)
100:     df.loc[censored, "duration_min"] = np.nan
101:     return df.sort_values(["region", "started_at"]).reset_index(drop=True)
102: 
103: 
104: def load_alerts(path: str | pathlib.Path = DEFAULT_ALERTS) -> pd.DataFrame:
105:     """Cached, read-only alert table: `region, started_at, finished_at, naive, duration_min`."""
106:     return _load_alerts_cached(str(path))
107: 
108: 
109: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=None)
110: def load_weather(directory: str | pathlib.Path = DEFAULT_WEATHER) -> pd.DataFrame:
111:     """Cached, read-only hourly weather for every region (`time` UTC-aware)."""
112:     frames = []
113:     for path in sorted(pathlib.Path(directory).glob("*.csv")):
114:         df = pd.read_csv(path)
115:         for col in ("time", "sunrise", "sunset"):
116:             df[col] = pd.to_datetime(df[col], utc=True)
117:         df["region"] = SLUG_TO_REGION.get(path.stem, path.stem)
118:         frames.append(df)
119:     out = pd.concat(frames, ignore_index=True)
120:     return out.sort_values(["region", "time"]).reset_index(drop=True)
121: 
122: 
123: def hourly_grid(alerts: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
124:     """Dense `region × hour` grid: alert-minutes overlapping each hour and starts counts.
125: 
126:     The single source behind every lag and label: minutes split alert-seconds across the
127:     hour buckets it spans, starts counts alerts whose `started_at` floors into the hour.
128:     """
129:     a = alerts[["region", "started_at", "finished_at"]].reset_index(drop=True)
130: 
131:     def naive(s: pd.Series) -> np.ndarray:
132:         return s.dt.tz_convert("UTC").dt.tz_localize(None).to_numpy()
133: 
134:     start, finish = naive(a["started_at"]), naive(a["finished_at"])
135:     start_h = a["started_at"].dt.floor("h")
136:     end_h = a["finished_at"].dt.floor("h")
137:     n = (((end_h - start_h).dt.total_seconds() // 3600).astype("int64") + 1).clip(lower=1)
138:     n_arr = n.to_numpy()
139:     idx = np.repeat(np.arange(len(a)), n_arr)
140:     offs = np.arange(len(idx)) - np.repeat(np.cumsum(n_arr) - n_arr, n_arr)
141:     hour = naive(start_h)[idx] + offs * np.timedelta64(1, "h")
142:     minutes = (np.minimum(finish[idx], hour + np.timedelta64(1, "h")) - np.maximum(start[idx], hour))
143:     minutes = np.clip(minutes / np.timedelta64(1, "m"), 0.0, None)
144: 
145:     agg = pd.DataFrame(
146:         {"region": a["region"].to_numpy()[idx], "hour": hour, "minutes": minutes}
147:     ).groupby(["region", "hour"], as_index=False)["minutes"].sum()
148:     agg["hour"] = pd.to_datetime(agg["hour"], utc=True)
149:     starts = (
150:         a.assign(hour=start_h)
151:         .groupby(["region", "hour"], as_index=False)
152:         .size()
153:         .rename(columns={"size": "starts"})
154:     )
155: 
156:     regions = sorted(a["region"].unique())
157:     hours = pd.date_range(start_h.min(), end_h.max(), freq="h")
158:     grid = pd.DataFrame({"region": regions}).merge(pd.DataFrame({"hour": hours}), how="cross")
159:     grid = grid.merge(agg, on=["region", "hour"], how="left").merge(starts, on=["region", "hour"], how="left")
160:     grid["minutes"] = grid["minutes"].fillna(0.0)
161:     grid["starts"] = grid["starts"].fillna(0).astype("int64")
162:     return grid.sort_values(["region", "hour"]).reset_index(drop=True)
163: 
164: 
165: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=None)
166: def load_grid(path: str | pathlib.Path = DEFAULT_ALERTS) -> pd.DataFrame:
167:     """Cached hourly grid; keeps ~90 backtest origins from rebuilding ~1M grid rows."""
168:     return hourly_grid(load_alerts(path))
169: 
170: 
171: def daily_origin(day) -> pd.Timestamp:
172:     """00:00 Europe/Kyiv on `day` (str/date/Timestamp) as a UTC-aware Timestamp."""
173:     ts = pd.Timestamp(day)
174:     ts = ts.tz_localize(KYIV) if ts.tzinfo is None else ts.tz_convert(KYIV)
175:     return ts.normalize().tz_convert("UTC")
176: 
177: 
178: def origins(start_day, end_day) -> list[pd.Timestamp]:
179:     """Inclusive daily origins over `[start_day, end_day]` (Europe/Kyiv midnight, UTC)."""
180:     days = pd.date_range(pd.Timestamp(start_day).date(), pd.Timestamp(end_day).date(), freq="D")
181:     return [daily_origin(day) for day in days]
182: 
183: 
184: def _lags(alerts: pd.DataFrame, grid: pd.DataFrame, origin: pd.Timestamp, regions: list[str]) -> pd.DataFrame:
185:     past = _guard(grid[grid["hour"] < origin].copy(), "hour", origin)
186:     past_alerts = _guard(alerts[alerts["started_at"] < origin].copy(), "started_at", origin)
187: 
188:     out = pd.DataFrame(index=pd.Index(regions, name="region"))
189:     for name, span in (
190:         ("starts_past_1h", "1h"),
191:         ("starts_past_6h", "6h"),
192:         ("starts_past_24h", "24h"),
193:         ("starts_past_72h", "72h"),
194:     ):
195:         window = past[past["hour"] >= origin - pd.Timedelta(span)]
196:         out[name] = window.groupby("region")["starts"].sum().reindex(out.index, fill_value=0)
197: 
198:     window = past[past["hour"] >= origin - pd.Timedelta("24h")]
199:     out["alert_minutes_past_24h"] = window.groupby("region")["minutes"].sum().reindex(out.index, fill_value=0.0)
200: 
201:     window = past[past["hour"] >= origin - pd.Timedelta("28D")]
202:     out["trailing_28d_starts_per_day"] = (
203:         window.groupby("region")["starts"].sum().reindex(out.index, fill_value=0) / 28.0
204:     )
205: 
206:     last = past_alerts.groupby("region")["started_at"].max()
207:     since = (origin - last).dt.total_seconds() / 3600.0
208:     out["hours_since_last_alert"] = since.reindex(out.index).clip(upper=MAX_HOURS_SINCE)
209: 
210:     # Mass attacks are nationwide, so how many regions are alerting right now leads each region.
211:     window = past[past["hour"] >= origin - pd.Timedelta("3h")]
212:     out["regions_alerting_past_3h"] = int(window.loc[window["minutes"] > 0, "region"].nunique())
213:     return out.reset_index()
214: 
215: 
216: def _weather_lags(weather: pd.DataFrame, origin: pd.Timestamp, regions: list[str]) -> pd.DataFrame:
217:     past = _guard(weather[weather["time"] < origin].copy(), "time", origin)
218:     tail = past.sort_values(["region", "time"]).groupby("region").tail(24)
219:     agg = tail.groupby("region").agg(
220:         temp_mean_24h=("temperature_2m", "mean"),
221:         cloud_mean_24h=("cloud_cover", "mean"),
222:         wind_mean_24h=("wind_speed_10m", "mean"),
223:         precip_sum_24h=("precipitation", "sum"),
224:     )
225:     return agg.reindex(regions).reset_index()
226: 
227: 
228: def _daylight(weather: pd.DataFrame, rows: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
229:     """Minutes-to-sunset / night flag / day length per forecast hour.
230: 
231:     Sunrise and sunset are deterministic astronomy known years ahead, so this family is
232:     intentionally NOT routed through `_guard` even though the weather file carries them.
233:     """
234:     day = (
235:         weather.assign(date=weather["time"].dt.tz_convert("UTC").dt.normalize())
236:         .groupby(["region", "date"], as_index=False)[["sunrise", "sunset"]]
237:         .first()
238:         .sort_values(["region", "date"])
239:     )
240:     day[["sunrise", "sunset"]] = day.groupby("region")[["sunrise", "sunset"]].ffill()
241: 
242:     out = rows[["region", "hour"]].copy()
243:     out["date"] = out["hour"].dt.normalize()
244:     out = out.merge(day, on=["region", "date"], how="left")
245:     for col in ("sunrise", "sunset"):
246:         tod = out[col].dt.hour * 60 + out[col].dt.minute + out[col].dt.second / 60.0
247:         median_tod = tod.groupby(out["region"]).transform("median")  # fallback for gap dates
248:         out[col] = out[col].fillna(out["date"] + pd.to_timedelta(median_tod, unit="m"))
249: 
250:     return pd.DataFrame(
251:         {
252:             "region": out["region"],
253:             "hour": out["hour"],
254:             "minutes_to_sunset": (out["sunset"] - out["hour"]).dt.total_seconds() / 60.0,
255:             "is_night": ((out["hour"] < out["sunrise"]) | (out["hour"] > out["sunset"])).astype(int),
256:             "day_length_min": (out["sunset"] - out["sunrise"]).dt.total_seconds() / 60.0,
257:         }
258:     )
259: 
260: 
261: def _labels(alerts: pd.DataFrame, grid: pd.DataFrame, rows: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
262:     """Start flag, alert-minutes and log-duration-at-start per hour. Never guarded."""
263:     out = rows[["region", "hour"]].merge(
264:         grid[["region", "hour", "minutes", "starts"]].rename(columns={"minutes": "y_minutes", "starts": "y_start"}),
265:         on=["region", "hour"],
266:         how="left",
267:     )
268:     out["y_minutes"] = out["y_minutes"].fillna(0.0)
269:     out["y_start"] = out["y_start"].fillna(0).gt(0).astype(int)
270: 
271:     first = (
272:         alerts.assign(hour=alerts["started_at"].dt.floor("h"))
273:         .sort_values(["region", "hour", "started_at"])
274:         .drop_duplicates(["region", "hour"], keep="first")
275:         .assign(y_dur_log=lambda d: np.log1p(d["duration_min"]))  # NaN when censored/>24h
276:     )
277:     return out.merge(first[["region", "hour", "y_dur_log"]], on=["region", "hour"], how="left")
278: 
279: 
280: def build(
281:     origin: pd.Timestamp,
282:     alerts: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
283:     weather: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
284:     grid: pd.DataFrame | None = None,
285: ) -> pd.DataFrame:
286:     """Feature matrix for one origin: 24 region × hour rows with lags and labels.
287: 
288:     Pass nothing for the fast cached path (backtests); pass fixtures for tests. All loaded
289:     tables are treated as read-only.
290:     """
291:     origin = _as_utc(origin)
292:     if alerts is None and grid is None:
293:         grid = load_grid()
294:     alerts = load_alerts() if alerts is None else alerts
295:     weather = load_weather() if weather is None else weather
296:     grid = hourly_grid(alerts) if grid is None else grid
297: 
298:     regions = sorted(alerts["region"].unique())
299:     hours = pd.date_range(origin, periods=24, freq="h")
300:     rows = pd.DataFrame({"region": regions}).merge(pd.DataFrame({"hour": hours}), how="cross")
301: 
302:     local = rows["hour"].dt.tz_convert(KYIV)
303:     rows["hour_local"] = local.dt.hour.astype(int)
304:     rows["day_of_week"] = local.dt.dayofweek.astype(int)
305:     rows["is_weekend"] = (local.dt.dayofweek >= 5).astype(int)
306:     rows["month"] = local.dt.month.astype(int)
307:     rows["is_holiday"] = local.dt.strftime("%m-%d").isin(HOLIDAYS).astype(int)
308: 
309:     out = (
310:         rows.merge(_lags(alerts, grid, origin, regions), on="region", how="left")
311:         .merge(_weather_lags(weather, origin, regions), on="region", how="left")
312:         .merge(_daylight(weather, rows), on=["region", "hour"], how="left")
313:         .merge(_labels(alerts, grid, rows), on=["region", "hour"], how="left")
314:     )
315:     out.insert(0, "origin", origin)
316:     out = out[["origin", "region", "hour", *FEATURES, "y_start", "y_minutes", "y_dur_log"]]
317:     return out.sort_values(["region", "hour"]).reset_index(drop=True)

So the guard is clean for all 17 non-daylight features. However, this behavior is fragile: the daylight features silently depend on weather file coverage. For evaluation origins whose horizon extends beyond the weather file (e.g. the last origin today, horizon to 20:00 UTC today is covered, fine), it's OK. But if someone evaluates an origin at the very end of data where horizon exceeds coverage, daylight falls back to previous-day values — a few minutes off, harmless.

I could make daylight robust by not depending on the weather file at all — e.g., compute from the file where available but explicitly extend: for horizon hours beyond coverage, use previous day's sunrise/sunset + 1 day (astronomy changes ~1-2 min/day). That's a small improvement. Or simply accept: documented as astronomy.

Given "lazy senior dev", the fallback is acceptable and documented. `git status` shows only `src/models.py` + `tests/test_models.py` added by me; `src/eda.py`, `tests/test_eda.py`, `reports/` are the other agent's and untouched.

**Design decisions worth knowing**
- `DAY_FEATURES` = `FEATURES` minus `hour_local`/`minutes_to_sunset`/`is_night` (hour-specific; they'd encode the arbitrary "first hour" of the day). `day_of_week`, `is_weekend`, `month`, `is_holiday`, `day_length_min` are kept because they describe the origin's local calendar day.
- `fit_predict` selects the `FEATURES` columns present in the frame, asserting the set is exactly `FEATURES` or `DAY_FEATURES` — one code path for hourly frames and daily panels, no silent under-fitting. No scaler.
- Both linear models use `solver="newton-cholesky"`: raw lag features make lbfgs take ~3900 iters / 39 s on real data (and it still hit the 1000-iter limit); newton-cholesky converges in ~10 iters / 0.1 s, deterministic, no scaler needed. Documented with a `ponytail:` note (needs features < rows).
- Linear models reject NaN, and `hours_since_last_alert` is NaN for the first days of the series, so `fit_predict` fills NaN with the **train** median (imputer fit on train only; HistGB takes NaN natively). `duration_naive` returns 0.0 instead of NaN when the train window has no duration rows.
- `hour_of_week_profile` carries `p_region`/`p_global` as broadcast columns; `predict_hour_of_week` reads them via a separate map (a left join would NaN them out on unseen cells — caught by a test).

**Tests:** `uv run pytest -q` → `28 passed in 6.3s` (14 new, 14 pre-existing).

- 576 rows/origin = 24 regions × 24 h; concat 5184×26; 0 NaN in `FEATURES`; `daily_panel` rows = **216 == 9 origins × 24 regions**, no duplicate keys; targets `y_starts_day∈[0,17]`, `y_minutes_day∈[0,574]`, `y_dur_log∈[0.17,6.08]`.
- Model 1: Poisson `y_starts_day` 48 finite ≥0 preds; Poisson `y_minutes_day`; Logistic `y_start` 1152 preds in [0,1]. No warnings (convergence warnings promoted to errors).
- Model 2: HistGB poisson both targets; HistGB quantile 0.1/0.5/0.9 on 248 duration rows, finite and ordered q10≤q50≤q90.
- Baselines fine; MAE on the tiny split: `y_start` A/B 0.315 vs logistic 0.209; `y_starts_day` Poisson 1.65 vs HistGB 1.39; `y_dur_log` naive 0.850 vs q50 0.821.

```json
{
```
```json
  "path": "/home/agent/Code/KSE-Agentic-AI-stage2/src/models.py"
```
